## NCT06256419
Department of Pharmacy, the Affiliated Hospital of Xuzhou Medical University

Intervention: : GLP-1 receptor agonist — Eligible patients with T2DM were required to have received GLP-1RA as monotherapy or in combination with other antidiabetic agents. GLP-1 RA was injected subcutaneously at standard dose and frequency for consecutive 6 months in patients with T2DM.
: responders group and nonresponders group — For all the patients with type 2 diabetes who were initially enrolled in the study, blood samples were obtained for genotyping before the administration of GLP-1 receptor agonists. Patients were re-screened according to whether they had used GLP-1 RA continuously for more than 6 months and had completed the specified follow-up tasks.

Patients were divided according to the type of T2DM susceptibility genes. Or all were divided into responses group and nonresponses group according to whether they had glycemic response (△HbA1c↓ ≥1.0%) and weight response (△weight↓ ≥3.0%) after taking GLP-1 receptor agonist for 6 months.

According to the above grouping, the variables that can predict the efficacy of the drug were identified, and the weight of the influence of these variables on the efficacy was evaluated.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06256419

SUMMARY:

## NCT07858682
Accuracy of the Calibrated Framework for Intraoral Full-Arch Scanning Compared With Conventional Splinted Open-Tray Impression in Edentulous Patients: A Crossover Clinical Trial

Intervention: : Conventional Splinted Open-Tray Implant Impression — A conventional verified splinted open-tray implant impression will be performed after implant placement. Multi-unit impression copings will be splinted using dental floss and autopolymerizing resin, followed by a polyvinyl siloxane (PVS) impression. The master cast will be fabricated, verified, and scanned with a laboratory scanner to generate an STL file for accuracy analysis.
: Intraoral Digital Scanning Using a Calibrated Framework — An intraoral digital impression will be obtained after implant placement using an intraoral scanner and a calibrated stock framework attached to implant scan bodies. The resulting STL dataset will be compared with the conventional impression using Geomagic Control X software to evaluate linear, angular, and global deviations in implant position transfer accuracy.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07858682

Intervention: : GLP-1 receptor agonist (e.g., semaglutide, tirzepatide) - prescribed by the treating physician as standard care; the study does not assign or alter treatment. — GLP-1 receptor agonist (GLP-1 RA) or dual GLP-1/GIP receptor agonist (e.g., semaglutide, tirzepatide) prescribed by the treating physician as routine clinical care for obesity in adults without diabetes. The agent, dose and duration are determined by the treating physician; the study does not assign, supply or modify treatment. Participants are enrolled at GLP-1 RA initiation and followed for 3 months at the Diabetes Education Unit. Study-specific assessments at baseline and month 3: SARC-F questionnaire, handgrip strength (hydraulic dynamometer), five-times chair stand test and 4-metre gait speed. ## NCT07860203
Effects of Stellate Ganglion Block Combined With Dexmedetomidine on Postoperative Sleep Quality in Patients Undergoing Laparoscopic Gynecological Surgery: A Randomized Controlled 2×2 Factorial Trial

Intervention: : DEX 0.2 μg/kg/h. — Dexmedetomidine is administered by intravenous infusion after induction of anesthesia without a loading dose, at an infusion rate of 0.2μg·kg-¹·h-¹, and continued until the end of surgery.
: SGB — Stellate ganglion block (SGB) is performed 15 min after induction of anesthesia by an anesthesiologist experienced in ultrasound-guided regional anesthesia, who is aware of the patient's group assignment. The patient is placed in the supine position with the neck slightly extended and rotated to the contralateral side. Under sterile conditions, a high-frequency linear-array ultrasound transducer is used to scan the anterior neck transversely at the C6 level, sequentially identifying the common carotid artery, internal jugular vein, thyroid gland, esophagus, C6 transverse process and anterior tubercle, longus colli muscle, and surrounding vascular structures.

In the SGB group, an in-plane needle approach is used under real-time ultrasound guidance. After the needle tip reaches the fascial plane anterior to the longus colli muscle and negative aspiration for blood is confirmed, 5 mL of 0.375% ropivacaine is injected slowly. Spread of the local anesthetic along the surface of the longus
: 0.9% Sodium Chloride (Placebo) — An equal volume of 0.9% sodium chloride solution is administered by intravenous infusion after induction of anesthesia as a placebo for dexmedetomidine and continued until the end of surgery.
: Sham Stellate Ganglion Block — Participants are placed in the same position as those receiving the stellate ganglion block, and the ultrasound probe is placed on the neck in an identical manner to simulate the procedure. No needle puncture or drug injection is performed.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07860203

SUMMARY:

## NCT07860216
Phase III Multicenter, Randomized, Blinded, Placebo-controlled Trial Assessing the Efficacy, Safety, and Immunogenicity of Recombinant RSV Vaccine (CHO Cell) in Adults Aged ≥60 Years

Intervention: : Recombinant respiratory syncytial virus vaccine (CHO cell) — Test vaccine BW-201 (Adj 1): After mixing the "recombinant respiratory syncytial virus vaccine adjuvant injection" upside down, fully aspirate and inject it into the "RSV vaccine" lyophilized powder for re-dissolution. Slowly rotate the vial to mix, and use it after complete dissolution.

After mixing the antigen and adjuvant, store them in a refrigerator at 2°C-8°C in the dark and use them within 4 hours. Before use, they need to be mixed again. If the mixture of antigen and adjuvant is left at room temperature (not exceeding 25°C), it needs to be used within 30 minutes.

After mixing 1 bottle of adjuvant solution according to the above steps, aspirate the entire content and add it to the antigen, followed by mixing. Each person should receive 0.5mL per injection, administered intramuscularly into the deltoid muscle on the lateral side of the upper arm. Intravenous injection is strictly prohibited.
: Sodium Chloride Injection 0.9% — The placebo is a 0.9% sodium chloride solution. Each person is to receive 0.5mL for intramuscular injection, with the injection site being the deltoid muscle on the lateral side of the upper arm. Intravenous injection is strictly prohibited. The remaining 0.9% sodium chloride solution (approximately 9.5mL) should be disposed of as waste.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07860216

SUMMARY:

Treatments involve one-dimensional (lateral-lateral or anteroposterior) and two-dimensional active mobilization of the upper extremities using the PABLO® Multiboard, a device that secures the hand and wrist with a handle and allows for flexion and extension movements of the elbow and shoulder.

Treatments are designed to maintain or restore range of motion in the shoulder joint through passive, active-assisted, and active mobilization exercises guided by the physical therapist, strength training of the proximal muscles of the upper limb, electrostimulation of the proximal muscles of the upper limb and use of physical therapies for analgesic and anti-inflammatory purposes.

Intervention: : Rehabilitation treatment with IVS before step training using an exoskeleton — * 15 minutes of IVS: The physical therapist will show the patient a pre-recorded sequence of movements and activities involving the lower limb. Patient will observe the movements on a screen positioned to cover the paretic limb and will then be ask to repeat them.
* 30 minutes of EKSO training:The physical therapist will set up the training using a single program defined as "assistive" to observe the degree of dependence on the machine throughout the course of treatment and will maintain this setting for all sessions, documenting the percentage changes in the level of assistance required.
* 45 minutes of non-robotic motor therapy following the robotic treatment. The physical therapist will perform motor treatments involving mobilization, muscle strengthening, and standing with the goal of improving the patient's ability to perform transfers and maintain an upright position independently.

The rehabilitation treatment session will be repeated 5 days a week for 3 weeks.

## NCT07861295
Shatavari & Ashoka for Menopause (SAM) Study

Intervention: : Ashoka Bark and Shatavari — The intervention is an oral botanical dietary supplement containing Ashoka Bark (Saraca asoca) and Shatavari (Asparagus racemosus) intended for the management of menopausal symptoms. Participants will consume one capsule daily (100 mg) before or during a meal for 12 weeks. The formulation is being evaluated for its potential effects on menopausal symptom severity, hormone-related measures, quality of life, and related health outcomes in postmenopausal women. The intervention will be compared with a matched placebo under randomized, double-blind study conditions. Safety, adherence, and tolerability will be monitored throughout the study period.
: Placebo — Matched placebo capsule containing inactive ingredients, administered orally once daily before or during a meal for 12 weeks. The placebo is identical in appearance to the active Ashoka Bark and Shatavari supplement to maintain blinding of participants, investigators, and outcome assessors. The placebo contains no active botanical ingredients and is used as the comparator to evaluate the effects of the Ashoka Bark and Shatavari intervention on menopausal symptoms and related outcomes.

## NCT07861321
A Randomized Controlled Trial to Evaluate the Cariostatic Efficacy of Nano Silver Fluoride (NSF) vs. Silver Diamine Fluoride (SDF) in Children

Intervention: : Nano Silver Fluoride / NSF — The intervention consists of a single topical application of either ClearDefense® Nano Silver Fluoride (3% sodium fluoride + 0.2% silver ion) or AdvantageArrest® Silver Diamine Fluoride (38% SDF) directly onto active carious lesions on primary molars or first permanent molars. Prior to treatment, visible plaque is removed using a tapered brush prophy angle, baseline tooth shade is recorded, and the target teeth are isolated with cotton rolls and gauze before being dried with an air-water syringe. To maintain blinding, a study coordinator dispenses two drops of the assigned agent into a dappen dish, and the study investigator applies the product across the entire carious lesion using a microbrush, allowing it to sit for at least one minute while soft tissues are retracted to avoid staining. Finally, excess product is wiped away with gauze or a cotton roll, and participants are instructed to refrain from eating or drinking for at least 30 minutes post-application.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07861321

## NCT07861958
Improving the Acceptability of Wrist Orthoses Through a Co-design Process With Pediatric Patients

Intervention: : Orthosis acceptability — Participants will receive a personalized wrist-hand orthosis developed through a co-design process. During two structured workshops, children and caregivers express aesthetic preferences and personalization needs using creative tools (drawings, collage, 3D-printed prototypes). Upper limb anatomy is acquired via 3D scanning and/or anthropometric measurements. The orthosis is then digitally designed, 3D-printed with biocompatible materials using a multicolor FDM printer, finished manually, and certified as a custom medical device per MDR 745/2017. Participants evaluate the co-designed orthosis in terms of acceptability (TAM-3), satisfaction (QUEST 2.1), and upper limb functional performance (optoelectronic motion analysis), comparing outcomes with their conventional orthosis assessed at baseline.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07861958

## NCT07862010
An Exploratory Clinical Study Evaluating GI001 in the Treatment of Relapsed/Refractory B-Cell Non-Hodgkin Lymphoma and B-Cell Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia

Intervention: : GI001 — GI001 is an in vivo CD19-targeted CAR-T therapy. It is a lentiviral vector-based product using a third-generation self-inactivating lentiviral vector system with a five-plasmid packaging design. GI001 is administered by intravenous injection and delivers the CD19 CAR gene directly into the patient, reprogramming the patient's own T cells in situ to express CD19 CAR without ex vivo cell manufacturing. No lymphodepleting conditioning is required, and a single injection is given. GI001 is supplied as a colorless to slightly opaque liquid, 1 mL per vial, stored at -70 +/- 10 degrees C. Infusion duration does not exceed 2 hours. Dose levels evaluated in this study are 3.5x10\^8 TU, 7.0x10\^8 TU and 1.0x10\^9 TU (TU = transducing units). Recommended premedication is intramuscular promethazine 25 mg or diphenhydramine 20 mg given 15-30 minutes before infusion. Dose modification is not permitted (a +/-20% dose range is acceptable).
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07862010

## NCT07863128
The UNCOVER Study: From ICU to Long-Term Recovery - An International Cohort Study on Risk Factors, Trajectories, and Quality of Life in Sepsis Survivors

Intervention: : Care for Sepsis — Participants will receive standard clinical care for sepsis according to local hospital protocols.

This study is purely observational: no experimental drugs, procedures, or behavioral interventions are introduced.

Data will be collected prospectively on infection characteristics, timing of adequate antibiotic therapy, organ support, and rehabilitation practices during the ICU stay.

Follow-up at 28 days, 3 months, and 6 months will assess patient-centered outcomes such as quality of life (EQ-5D-5L), cognitive and mental health status, and return to work.

All treatments and decisions remain under the responsibility of the attending medical team.

## NCT07863583
Intervention Pilot: Identifying and Addressing Social Isolation Among Older Adults Living in Subsidized Housing

Intervention: : Social Connection Collective: Open-Label Pilot — The Social Connection Collective is a resident-led intervention to promote social connection and reduce social isolation among older adults in subsidized housing. Approximately 6-10 Resident Champions at each site will receive research team support to develop and implement community activities and peer-support opportunities through approximately six co-design sessions over six months. The intervention is adapted to each community's needs and resources, with an emphasis on resident leadership and sustainability. Investigators will further refine the intervention based on feedback from open-label pilot participants and the study team who implemented the pilot, resulting in an adapted iteration of the intervention.
: Social Connection Collective: Pilot — The Social Connection Collective is a resident-led intervention to promote social connection and reduce social isolation among older adults in subsidized housing. Approximately 6-10 Resident Champions at each site will receive research team support to develop and implement community activities and peer-support opportunities through approximately six structured co-design sessions over six months. The intervention is adapted to each community's needs, interests, and resources, with an emphasis on resident leadership and sustainability. Refinements based on open-label pilot results will be included.

## NCT07863908
Sepsis and Acute Respiratory Distress Syndrome in China (SEARCH): A Multicenter Prospective Observational Study of Clinical and Biological Phenotypes

Intervention: : Surveys — Participants will complete the EuroQol-5 Dimension-5 Level (EQ-5D-5L) quality of life assessment in person. Assessments will be conducted at 1, 6, 12, and 24 month(s) following hospitalization. For participants who do not attend in-person follow-up visits, surveys will be conducted via email, text message, or phone.
: 6-Minute Walk Test — The patient sits at rest while baseline vital signs and exertion scores are taken.The patient then walks back and forth along a flat, marked corridor at their own pace for 6 minutes, followed immediately by repeating vital signs and exertion measurements at the finish.

At visits 1, 6, 12 and 24 month(s) following hospitalization.
: Blood collection — Venous blood is collected via direct needle stick into a vein. Arterial blood is collected by a direct needle puncture into an artery (typically the radial artery) using a heparinized syringe.

At visits 1, 6, 12 and 24 month(s) following hospitalization.
: Pulmonary & Respiratory Muscle Function Testing — The patient sits upright, wears a nose clip, and breathes through a mouthpiece connected to the testing device, performing guided forced breathing and single-breath hold maneuvers. Respiratory muscle strength is measured by having the patient inhale and exhale forcefully against a temporarily blocked mouthpiece.

At visits 1, 6, 12 and 24 month(s) following hospitalization.
: CT Scan — The patient lies flat on the CT scanner bed and follows automated breathing prompts to hold their breath for a few seconds while images are acquired. Additional body regions may be scanned if clinically indicated.

At visits 1, 6, 12 and 24 month(s) following hospitalization.
: Blood Collection — Blood samples will be collected on Day 1, Day 3, and Day 7. Day 1 samples will be collected within 48 hours after ARDS diagnosis. Day 7 samples can be collected between Day 6 and Day 8.

Plasma、 PBMCs and Serum will be collected in each corresponding tube and will be separated and stored for later testing.

Citrate plasma will be collected in sodium citrate tubes and then will be centrifuged within 1 hour, and the plasma will be stored at -80°C for later testing.
: Bronchoalveolar Lavage Fluid — Bronchoalveolar lavage fluid (BALF) will be collected on Day 1, Day 3, and Day 7 and stored for later testing.
: HME filter samples — HME filter samples will be collected on Day 1, Day 3, and Day 7 and stored for later testing.

If the patient is extubated, dies, or leaves the hospital before the planned BALF or HME sample collection, the last sample will be collected before or on the same day.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07863908

SUMMARY:

## NCT07863960
Efficacy and Safety of Photobiomodulation on Postoperative Pain in Postpartum Women Undergoing Cesarean Section at Hospital Escuela and IHSS Tegucigalpa, Honduras: A Randomized, Double-Blind, Sham-Controlled Clinical Trial

Intervention: : Photobiomodulation Therapy (MitoQUAD® Belt System) — The Sham Photobiomodulation Device is an inactive placebo unit designed to serve as a control intervention. It is physically identical to the active MitoQUAD® Belt system in appearance, size, shape, weight, color, timer display, audible signals, and user operation, but delivers no therapeutic red or near-infrared light energy. The sham device is applied transcutaneously over a sterile transparent dressing covering the Pfannenstiel surgical wound using the exact same treatment schedule:Session 1: Applied approximately 30 minutes post-cesarean delivery. Session 2: Applied at 24 hours post-surgery. Session 3: Applied between 36 and 48 hours post-surgery. Each session lasts 15 minutes. Participants in this control arm receive identical standard institutional post-cesarean multimodal analgesia. Application procedures, duration, and patient positioning are indistinguishable from the experimental group to maintain study blinding.
: Sham Photobiomodulation Device — The Sham Photobiomodulation Device is an inactive placebo unit designed to serve as a control intervention. It is physically identical to the active MitoQUAD® Belt system in appearance, size, shape, weight, color, timer display, audible signals, and user operation, but delivers no therapeutic red or near-infrared light energy.

The sham device is applied transcutaneously over a sterile transparent dressing covering the Pfannenstiel surgical wound using the exact same treatment schedule:

Session 1: Applied approximately 30 minutes post-cesarean delivery.

Session 2: Applied at 24 hours post-surgery.

Session 3: Applied between 36 and 48 hours post-surgery.

Each session lasts 15 minutes. Participants in this control arm receive identical standard institutional post-cesarean multimodal analgesia. Application procedures, duration, and patient positioning are indistinguishable from the experimental group to maintain study blinding.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07863960

## NCT07864389
Proof of Concept Study to Evaluate the Feasibility of a Multimodal Approach Combining Angiography and 3D Ultrasound for the Hemodynamic Analysis of Unruptured, Untreated Intracranial Aneurysms

Intervention: : 3D CEUS — SYLVER is the investigational, non-invasive, class IIa ultrasound imaging system providing three-dimensional contrast-enhanced ultrasound (3D CEUS) imaging of the cerebral vasculature through the skull. Transcranial 3D CEUS acquisition is performed via the temporal acoustic window during the day-hospitalization for standard-of-care follow-up cerebral angiography, for a maximum of 30 minutes. Acquisitions are used to assess aneurysm presence, location and shape, intrasaccular flow organization (jet, recirculation, stagnation), filling completeness, and time-intensity curve and flow velocity parameters (time of arrival, time to peak, pulsatility index), which are compared with 3D rotational angiography.

## NCT07865403
Feasibility and Preliminary Efficacy of Psychologically Informed Physiotherapy Delivered Via Telerehabilitation in People With Fibromyalgia: A Pilot Study

Intervention: : Online Psychologically-Informed Physical Therapy — The intervention consists of an individualized Psychologically-Informed Physical Therapy (PIPT) program delivered entirely remotely via Zoom. PIPT integrates physical rehabilitation with the identification and management of psychological and social factors that may influence pain perception, disability, and functional recovery, including fear of movement, dysfunctional beliefs, catastrophizing, and ineffective coping strategies. The intervention is not intended to treat primary psychological disorders; rather, it aims to help participants overcome psychosocial barriers to physical reactivation and develop effective self-management strategies.

The program includes 12 scheduled sessions, with up to 3 additional optional sessions, delivered over 12 months. The first session lasts approximately 60-75 minutes, while subsequent sessions last approximately 30-45 minutes. Eight sessions are delivered during the first 3 months, with the remaining sessions scheduled over the following 9 months
: Paper-based booklet — The control intervention consists of a paper-based Cognitive Behavioral Therapy booklet combined with a home exercise program. Participants will be instructed to perform the prescribed exercises three times per week during the first 3 months of the study.

During this period, participants will receive a weekly telephone call from a physiotherapist to monitor adherence to the exercise program. These calls will be limited to adherence monitoring and will not include additional education, coaching, or therapeutic interaction aimed at reproducing components of the PIPT intervention.

After the initial 3-month period, participants will receive additional follow-up telephone calls at 6 and 9 months after enrollment to monitor the progression of their condition. These contacts will likewise avoid patient education or therapeutic interaction intended to generate effects related to the therapeutic encounter.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07865403

SUMMARY:

## NCT07865546
Advanced Space-time Resolved Techniques for fUnctional neuROimaging Characterization of Structurally Abnormal Eloquent coRtical Areas

Intervention: : Functional neuroimaging — Novel advanced method based on Ultra High Field-MRI for personalised functional neuroimaging by depicting tiny neuronal MR signal fluctuations at high spatial and temporal resolution. Two approaches will be investigated:

1. Enhanced image quality for both structural and functional MRI through: a) DL-based denoising and sharpening reconstruction (arXiv:2008.06559) developed, tested and optimized for functional and connectivity studies; b) anti-aliasing DL reconstruction techniques (PMID: 31247254) implemented to achieve higher spatial and temporal resolution with respect to the current state of the art; c) field monitoring using the field camera developed and implemented to correct several dynamic field disturbances that result in image distortions and artifacts (PMID: 28077303).
2. Development of a novel, non-invasive, 3D whole-brain, direct functional neuroimaging method, with unprecedented sensitivity in order to resolve tiny neuronal MR signal fluctuations
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07865546

The programme is progressively adjusted according to performance and tolerance.
: Oregon Circuit Training — A supervised 8-week circuit-based conditioning programme delivered three times per week. Each approximately 45-minute session includes warm-up, a 30-minute multi-station circuit targeting strength, endurance, agility, and coordination, and cool-down. Work intervals are approximately 30 to 45 seconds with 15 to 30 seconds of rest, and training load is progressively increased.

## NCT07865793
Clinical Study of Three-dimensional Lattice Radiotherapy Combined With PD-1/PD-L1 Inhibitors in the Treatment of Advanced Solid Tumors

Intervention: : lattice radiotherapy and PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitors — For large tumors, Lattice spatial segmentation radiotherapy should be performed first (recommended 24Gy/8Gy/3f, 30Gy/10Gy/3f, 15Gy/15Gy/1f), with or without sequential conventional segmentation radiotherapy. The dose of sequential conventional segmentation radiotherapy mainly depends on the tumor size, actual condition, and OAR limit, for example: 20Gy/4Gy/5fx, 40-50Gy/2Gy/20-25fx. Receive PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor Q3W combined or not combined with systemic drug therapy within one month after radiotherapy (the combination drug regimen is determined according to the guidelines for each tumor type or the medication regimen recommended by the oncology department consultation. For patients with immune dysfunction, immune enhancing drugs such as thymidine can be used) until disease progression or intolerable treatment toxicity occurs (up to one year).
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07865793

SUMMARY:

## NCT07866040
Comparative Effectiveness of Biofeedback Exercises and the Mulligan Concept on Pain, Cervical Mobility, and Posture in Females With Mechanical Neck Pain: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Mulligan Upper Cervical Mobilization — Manual therapy using low-velocity, high-amplitude mobilization techniques based on the Mulligan concept. Translatoric mobilization movements are applied to restore mobility of the upper cervical joints: occipital-atlas (C0-C1), atlas-axis (C1-C2), and axis-C3 (C2-C3) segments. Techniques are tailored to each patient's clinical findings and follow IFOMPT recommendations. Techniques include upper cervical translatoric dorsal glide (C0-C1), ventral-cranial glide (C2-C3) in supine, and upper cervical translatoric dorsal glide (C1-C2).
: Conventional Cervical Exercises and Diaphragmatic Breathing — Standard cervical exercise program including diaphragmatic breathing exercises. Session preparation includes suboccipital release, myofascial release, and cervical muscle stretching (flexors, lateral flexors, extensors, and rotators 3 repetitions × 30 seconds each side) performed as warm-up and cool-down. Patients are instructed to perform self-treatment home exercises daily (2-5 times per day).
: Biofeedback-Guided Deep Cervical Flexor Training — Deep cervical flexor (DCF) retraining using the Stabilizer Pressure Biofeedback Unit (Chattanooga, TN, USA). Patient positioned in crook lying with the pressure sensor inflated to 30 mmHg beneath the occiput. The patient performs a slow nodding action to progressively target five pressure levels (2 mmHg increments up to 30 mmHg maximum), holding each contraction for 10 seconds with 3-5 second rest intervals. Protocol: 10 repetitions × 3 sets per session, 2-minute rest between sets, 3 days/week for 4 weeks. Exercise progression includes: bilateral DCF contraction in supine, bilateral DCF contraction in standing, unilateral contraction with cervical inclination in supine, unilateral contraction with cervical rotation in supine.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07866040

SUMMARY:

Intervention: : Neoadjuvant short-term radiotherapy and chemotherapy + iparomlimab and tuvonralimab — Short-course chemoradiotherapy was administered, with a total tumor dose of 25 Gy to the primary tumor and high-risk areas (5 Gy per session, 5 sessions in total). After completion of radiotherapy, a 2-week rest period was allowed. Following that, 4 cycles of Capoex combined with QL706 were given (every 3 weeks): On Day 1, QL706 was administered at 5 mg/kg via intravenous infusion; oxaliplatin was given at 130 mg/m² via intravenous infusion. : Neoadjuvant long-term radiotherapy and chemotherapy + iparomlimab and tuvonralimab — Long-term radiotherapy was administered to the primary tumor and high-risk areas, with a total dose of 50 Gy (2 Gy per session, 25 sessions in total). After completion of radiotherapy, a 2-week rest period was allowed. Following that, 4 cycles of Capoex combined with QL706 were administered (every 3 weeks): On Day 1, QL706 was given at a dose of 5 mg/kg via intravenous infusion; oxaliplatin was administered at a dose of 130 mg/m² via intravenous infusion from Day 1 to Day 14. ## NCT07866846
'Managing Pasireotide-associated Hyperglycaemia in Acromegaly With Eucaloric Very-low Carbohydrate Ketogenic Diet Versus Diabetes Medication: a Multicentre, Randomized, Open-label Proof-of-concept Study'

Intervention: : Ketogenic diet — Subjects randomized to the eucaloric very-low carbohydrate ketogenic diet start with an ad libitum ketogenic diet of which only the amount of carbohydrates is restricted (\<40 g of carbohydrate, approximately 155 g of fat, and approximately 115 g of protein per day). This amount of carbohydrates will lead to ketogenesis. Emphasis is placed on the use of products high in polyunsaturated and monounsaturated fatty acids (diet margarines, oils, fish, nuts), preferably with vegetable and marine sources of protein, in concordance with the national nutritional guidelines and the Mediterranean diet (11). We aim for a eucaloric diet, not restricting energy intake. For women we aim at 2000 kcal/day, for men 2500 kcal/day.
: Diabetes Medication — Subjects randomized to the diabetes medication arm will be treated with diabetic drugs according to the current Dutch guidelines, and the recently published consensus stategement for the treatment of pasireotide-induced hyperglycemia (outlined in Fig 2.) (22). Dose escalation or medication switches are made every 2-4 weeks at the discretion of the treating physician aiming at a normal Hba1c of \<44 mmol/L (Hba1c\<6.2%).

## NCT07867197
EFFECTIVENESS OF A DIGITAL HEALTH-GUIDED CARDIAC REHABILITATION PROGRAM ON FUNCTIONAL CAPACITY, AND QUALITY OF LIFE IN PATIENTS WITH MYOCARDIAL INFARCTION

Intervention: : Conventional Treatment group — Duration \& Frequency: A 12-week supervised program with 3 sessions per week for both groups.

Session Structure: Warm-up (5-10 min): Low-intensity stretching and rhythmic movements.

Aerobic Training (20-40 min): Treadmill exercise prescribed at 40%-60% Heart Rate Reserve (HRR) using the Karvonen formula.

Precision Intensity Target:

HR{max} is derived from a symptom-limited exercise stress test (rather than age-predicted formulas) to keep intensity safely below the patient's ischemic or symptomatic threshold.

Resistance \& Cool-down: Major muscle group resistance training (1-2 sets of 10-15 reps), cool-down exercises, and breathing techniques.

Patient Education: Covers risk-factor modification, medication adherence (e.g., beta-blockers), nutrition, and smoking cessation for secondary prevention.
: Digital and Conventional treatment — Treatment Procedure for the Digital Component:·

In addition to the conventional outpatient program, participants in the experimental group will receive a digital support package comprising:

An initial orientation session on application use, symptom reporting, exercise safety, and home monitoring.

A smartphone application and/or secure messaging platform for education, reminders, and communication.· A smart phone for step counting and activity monitoring.· Home-based walking or prescribed physical activity on non-clinic days, logged through the digital platform.· Weekly tele coaching or teleconsultation to review progress, symptoms, barriers, and goals.· Automated reminders and motivational messages to support adherence.· Clinician-reviewed adjustment of home activity targets based on symptoms, safety criteria, and monitored activity data.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07867197

SUMMARY:

## NCT07867392
Iparomlimab and Tuvonralimab in Combination With Albumin-Bound Paclitaxel, Oxaliplatin, and S-1 as Neoadjuvant Therapy for Locally Advanced Gastric or Gastroesophageal Junction Cancer: A Single-center, Single-arm, Prospective, Open-label, Phase 2 Clinical Study

Intervention: : Iparomlimab and Tuvonralimab (QL1706) — A recombined anti-PD-1/CTLA-4 bispecific antibody (development code QL1706) administered at a fixed dose of 5 mg/kg by intravenous infusion on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle for 4 cycles. This agent is distinguished from conventional immunotherapy by concurrently targeting both PD-1 and CTLA-4 inhibitory pathways within a single molecule, in contrast to sequential or separate use of a PD-1 inhibitor plus ipilimumab (CTLA-4 inhibitor). It is explored here in the neoadjuvant (pre-surgical) window rather than in the metastatic or adjuvant setting.
: nab-P-SOX（nab-paclitaxel, oxaliplatin, S-1） — A triplet chemotherapy backbone given on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle for 4 cycles: nab-paclitaxel 150 mg/m² IV, oxaliplatin 130 mg/m² IV, plus S-1 (tegafur/gimeracil/oteracil) orally 40-60 mg twice daily on Days 1-14 (dose by BSA). This regimen is distinguished from the more common SOX (S-1 + oxaliplatin) or mFOLFOX by the addition of albumin-bound paclitaxel to form a three-drug triplet, and by the inclusion of S-1, an oral fluoropyrimidine more commonly used in Asian gastric-cancer populations.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07867392

## NCT07867431
Effects of Incentive Spirometry Versus Diaphragmatic Breathing on Oxygen Saturation and Heart-rate Variability in Post Percutaneous Coronary Intervention Patients

Intervention: : Incentive Spirometry — Experimental: Incentive Spirometry Group A

Participants will perform incentive spirometry under physiotherapist supervision in addition to routine post-percutaneous coronary intervention care. They will sit comfortably, seal their lips around the mouthpiece, inhale slowly and deeply to raise the indicator to the prescribed target, briefly sustain inspiration, and return to normal breathing. Vital signs and oxygen saturation will be monitored before and after each session.

Post-PCI Day 0: 3 sessions/day, approximately 15 minutes/session, 10 repetitions/session.

Post-PCI Day 1: 3 sessions/day, approximately 20 minutes/session, 15 repetitions/session.
: Diaphragmatic Breathing — Active Comparator: Diaphragmatic Breathing Group B

Participants will perform diaphragmatic breathing under physiotherapist supervision in addition to routine post-percutaneous coronary intervention care. They will be positioned comfortably with the upper chest relaxed and instructed to inhale slowly through the nose while allowing the abdomen to rise, followed by slow, controlled exhalation through pursed lips while allowing the abdomen to fall. Vital signs and oxygen saturation will be monitored before and after each session.

Post-PCI Day 0: 3 supervised sessions/day, approximately 15 minutes/session, 10 repetitions/session.

Post-PCI Day 1: 3 supervised sessions/day, approximately 20 minutes/session, 15 repetitions/session.

Vital signs and oxygen saturation will be assessed \*\*before and after each session\*\*, and the participant will be monitored throughout the intervention for tolerance and any advers
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07867431

## NCT07867730
Effects of Inspiratory Muscle Training and Positive Expiratory Pressure on Mechanically Ventilated Restrictive Lung Disease Patients

Intervention: : Inspiratory muscle training — Inspiratory Muscle Training (IMT):

Patients will be positioned at a 45° angle in bed. The IMT device will be individually calibrated and connected to the endotracheal tube. Initial intensity will be set at 40% of the patient's maximal inspiratory pressure (MIP), progressing daily by 10%.

Positive Expiratory Pressure (PEP) Training:

Patients will be positioned laterally. The PEP device will be set at 7 cm H₂O resistance and will be connected to the endotracheal tube. Training will continue daily. Sessions will be paused if signs of respiratory or hemodynamic instability appear (e.g., altered respiratory rate, SpO₂, BP, HR, mental status, hemoptysis, arrhythmias, or diaphoresis). In such cases, training intensity will be maintained at the previous level.
: Conventional Chest Physical Therapy — Participants will receive standard physiotherapy consisting of passive and active range-of-motion exercises for the upper and lower limbs, chest physiotherapy (including chest percussion), and therapeutic positioning. Each treatment session will last 15 minutes and will be administered by a qualified physiotherapist according to the institutional standard of care.

## NCT07867951
Comparison of Mean Postoperative Pain After Using Double Antibiotic Paste Versus Calcium Hydroxide as an Intracanal Medicament in Teeth With Symptomatic Apical Periodontitis: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Calcium Hydroxide (Ca(OH)2) — Calcium hydroxide is a widely used intracanal medicament in endodontic treatment. It is a strong base with a high pH (approximately 12.5) that exhibits antibacterial activity against a broad spectrum of microorganisms. It is used to disinfect the root canal system, reduce residual bacteria after chemo-mechanical preparation, minimize postoperative inflammation, and address pain. In this study, calcium hydroxide will be placed into the root canals after chemo-mechanical preparation and drying with absorbent paper points. The access cavity will be sealed with temporary restorative material containing cotton pellets. The medicament will remain in the canal until the next appointment.
: Double Antibiotic Paste — Double antibiotic paste (DAP) is a mixture of metronidazole and ciprofloxacin. It is employed as an intracanal antibiotic to disinfect necrotic teeth. Its effectiveness has been demonstrated in reducing bacterial counts within infected root canals. In this study, DAP will be prepared by combining the contents of one FLAGYL® (metronidazole) 500 mg tablet and one Cipesta™ (ciprofloxacin) 500 mg tablet in a precise volume of normal saline to make a homogeneous paste. The paste will be placed into the root canals after chemo-mechanical preparation and drying with absorbent paper points. The access cavity will be sealed with temporary restorative material containing cotton pellets. The medicament will remain in the canal until the next appointment.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07867951

SUMMARY:

## NCT07868484
A Randomized, Double-Blind Controlled Trial Comparing the Effectiveness of Midpoint Transverse Process to Pleura (MTP) Block and Erector Spinae Plane (ESP) Block in Video-Assisted Thoracoscopic Surgery (VATS) at RS Ngoerah

Intervention: : Midpoint Transverse Process to Pleura (MTP) block — Performed under general anesthesia in the lateral decubitus position, at least 15 minutes before surgical incision. A linear transducer (10-15 MHz) is placed parasagittally 3 cm lateral to the midline at T4-T5, and the transverse process and pleura are identified. The midpoint between the posterior edge of the transverse process and the pleura is determined. A 22G echogenic needle is advanced in-plane from cranial to caudal until the tip reaches the midpoint. After 1 mL normal saline confirms fascial separation, ropivacaine 0.375% 20 mL is injected in increments with intermittent aspiration every 5 mL. Correct placement is confirmed by fascial spread and pleural displacement on real-time ultrasound. Performed by a consultant anesthesiologist (regional analgesia).
: Erector spinae plane (ESP) block — Performed under general anesthesia in the lateral decubitus position, at least 15 minutes before surgical incision. A linear transducer (10-15 MHz) is placed parasagittally 3 cm lateral to the midline at T4-T5, and the transverse process and the erector spinae muscle are identified. A 22G echogenic needle is advanced in-plane from caudal to cranial until the tip lies beneath the erector spinae muscle, just above the surface of the transverse process. A 1 mL normal saline injection produces the lift sign (erector spinae muscle lifted off the transverse process), then ropivacaine 0.375% 20 mL is injected gradually. Performed by a consultant anesthesiologist (regional analgesia).
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07868484

SUMMARY:

## NCT07869901
Feasibility Study of a Multicomponent Sleep and Circadian Rhythm Intervention in Surgical Intensive Care Unit Patients

Intervention: : Sound and Light Optimization — Patients receive eye masks and earplugs at night; Overhead lights are turned off at 10:00 p.m. and replaced with dim bedside lights until 7:00 a.m; Curtains are opened as early as possible after 7:00 a.m; Device alarms and telephones are set to low volume during the night and restored to normal levels after 7:00 a.m; Staff members switch their phones to silent or vibration mode and keep conversations at low volume; A noise meter is installed to remind staff to maintain a quiet environment.
: Night-time Patient Care Activities — Nighttime care activities will be reorganized to reduce patient disturbances. Non-urgent procedures will be postponed to daytime hours, while emergency interventions will proceed as needed.
: Enhanced Daytime Mobility — Additional early mobilization sessions led by a physiotherapist will be conducted once daily between 10:00 and 16:00 during ICU stay, including out-of-bed and limb exercises. The intensity will be individualized according to the patient's clinical condition.
: Cognitive Stimulation — Patients will receive daily orientation to time and environment to reinforce temporal and spatial awareness. Daytime interaction with patients will be encouraged to promote alertness and assess comfort and sleep quality. For nonverbal patients, visual aids such as picture cards or writing boards will be provided to facilitate communication. Simple entertainment tools (e.g., radio, rehabilitation ball) will be offered to promote wakefulness during the daytime.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07869901

## NCT07870265
Detection of the BRAF V600E Mutation in Liquid Biopsy Using the CRISPR/Cas12a System

Intervention: : Novel CRISPR/Cas12a Electrochemical Bioassay — Experimental diagnostic platform utilizing the CRISPR/Cas12a system for selective enzymatic degradation of wild-type (WT) DNA sequences, followed by isothermal nucleic acid amplification (LAMP) and end-point electrochemical signal detection on microelectrode chips. Designed for sensitive and specific detection of the BRAF V600E mutation in circulating cell-free DNA (cfDNA) isolated from plasma.
: Reference Standard Molecular Diagnostics (NGS and ddPCR) — Baseline baseline characterization of tumor tissue mutation status using Next-Generation Sequencing (NGS), accompanied by droplet digital PCR (ddPCR) for orthogonal measurement and quantitation of BRAF V600E allele frequency in circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA). Serves as the reference clinical comparator for evaluating sensitivity and specificity.
: Serial Peripheral Blood Sampling for Liquid Biopsy — Venipuncture collecting 10 mL of peripheral blood into dedicated blood collection tubes at two specified clinical timepoints: Day 1 (prior to treatment initiation) and Day 1 + 6 months during ongoing therapy. Collected blood samples undergo immediate plasma isolation and cfDNA extraction.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07870265

## NCT07871864
FOSTER Evolving Study Platform

Intervention: : Experimental group : Cabometyx ® 40mg/m2/day (Max: 60mg/day) , Treatment schedule : Daily**, day 1-28 during 4 weeks (ie. 1 cycle) during 12 cycles — The intervention evaluated in FOSTER-CabOS is a maintenance treatment strategy using cabozantinib administered after completion of standard first-line therapy for newly diagnosed high-grade osteosarcoma. Unlike induction or salvage treatments, this intervention is introduced in patients without disease progression following standard treatment and aims to prevent or delay relapse. The study specifically evaluates the added value of 12 cycles of cabozantinib as maintenance therapy in a broad population of paediatric and adult osteosarcoma patients. This distinguishes the intervention from other osteosarcoma studies that typically focus on chemotherapy intensification, relapse treatment, or biomarker-selected population.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07871864

SUMMARY:

All AI-assisted content is reviewed, clinically evaluated, and validated by healthcare professionals before being presented to participants to ensure accuracy, appropriateness, and consistency with clinical practice. Intervention: : Recombinant Humanized Anti-Interleukin-6 Receptor Monoclonal Antibody Injection — VDJ001 is a recombinant humanized monoclonal antibody targeting the interleukin-6 receptor (IL-6R). It is produced using Chinese hamster ovary (CHO) cells through DNA recombinant technology. The antibody specifically binds to both soluble and membrane-bound IL-6R (sIL-6R and mIL-6R), thereby inhibiting signal transduction mediated by sIL-6R or mIL-6R.

The placebo is manufactured to be identical in appearance, color, shape, size, taste, and packaging to the investigational product VDJ001 to maintain the integrity of the double-blind design. It is utilized solely as a control intervention for the purpose of this randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled clinical trial.

## NCT07873268
Prevalence of Molar-Incisor Hypomineralization (MIH) and Effectiveness of Resin Infiltration Treatment

Intervention: : Resin infiltration — Isolation \& Prophylaxis: Clean target enamel with fluoride-free paste. Apply rubber dam isolation to protect soft tissues.

Acid Etching: Apply Icon-Etch (15% HCl) for 2 minutes with light mechanical agitation. Rinse with water for \> 30 seconds and air-dry.

Desiccation \& Optical Verification: Apply Icon-Dry (99% ethanol) for 30 seconds and air-dry. Verify lesion masking visually; repeat etching step if opaque discoloration remains (up to 3 cycles).

First Infiltration: Apply Icon-Infiltrant for 3 minutes. Floss interproximally, remove excess resin with gentle air stream, and light-cure for 40 seconds.

Second Infiltration \& Finishing: Re-apply Icon-Infiltrant for 1 minute. Remove excess resin, light-cure for 40 seconds, remove isolation, and polish with fine finishing discs.
: Fluoride Varnishes — Teeth were cleaned with prophylaxis paste, isolated with cotton rolls, and dried. A thin layer of 5% sodium fluoride (NaF) varnish was applied using a microbrush. Patients were instructed to refrain from eating, drinking, or toothbrushing for at least 4 hours post-treatment to allow optimal remineralization. Follow-up evaluations were conducted at T1, T2, T3, T6, and T12 months.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07873268

SUMMARY:

: Moderate Intensity Continuous Group — Moderate-intensity continuous lower-extremity aerobic exercise training will be performed on a cycle ergometer for a total duration of 35 minutes. ## NCT07873606
Histotripsy for Pancreatic Cancer: a Novel Strategy for an Unmet Need

Intervention: : Histotripsy — Histotripsy is a promising non-invasive technique that uses high-intensity ultrasound waves to disrupt tissue without damaging surrounding structures. It works by using high-intensity ultrasound waves to create microscopic bubbles within the tumour tissue. These bubbles rapidly expand and collapse, generating shock waves that disrupt the tissue. The technique is highly precise and can be targeted to specific areas of the liver, allowing for selective destruction of tumour cells while minimizing damage to healthy tissue.

Other Name:

## NCT07874100
Analisi Geno-trascrittomica Dell'eterogeneità Clonale Nella Leucemia Mieloide Acuta a Livello di Singola Cellula: Implicazioni Per la Diagnosi e la Risposta al Trattamento Con Venetoclax

Intervention: : Bulk genotyping of AML samples at diagnosis and relapse, scRNA analysis at diagnosis, scRNAseq and genotyping analysis at day 28 after therapy, spatial transcriptomic analysis at day 0 and 28 — This intervention enrolls \~420 patients to investigate AML through multi-level genomic and transcriptomic analysis. Bone marrow samples are collected at diagnosis, relapse, and day 28 of treatment. All patients undergo bulk NGS genotyping using a 69-gene panel. Forty patients receiving venetoclax are selected for detailed single-cell RNAseq analysis (\~5000 cells) to dissect cellular heterogeneity, identify blast populations, assess microenvironment composition, and predict cell states via RNA velocity. Four patients undergo spatial transcriptomic analysis (Xenium) on bone marrow biopsies at diagnosis and day 28 to evaluate RNA expression within tissue architecture. This comprehensive approach enables identification of genomic/transcriptomic features associated with treatment response, chemoresistance mechanisms, and personalized targeted treatment markers in heterogeneous AML populations.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07874100

SUMMARY:

## NCT07874321
A Prospective, Open-Label, Single-Center, Multi-Cohort Phase II Study of Becotatug Vedotin (MRG003) Added to Ongoing Lenvatinib With or Without an Anti-PD-1 Antibody After Progression Following Prior Clinical Benefit in EGFR-Positive Advanced Thyroid Cancer

Intervention: : Becotatug Vedotin (MRG003) — MRG003 is added at 2.0 mg/kg intravenously on Day 1 of each 3-week cycle to the ongoing treatment backbone: lenvatinib plus the same anti-PD-1 inhibitor in Cohort A, or lenvatinib in Cohort B. MRG003 continues until subsequent disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, withdrawal of consent, or another protocol-defined discontinuation criterion; dose modifications follow protocol-defined safety rules. Infusion is over 60 +/- 15 minutes, with the first infusion lasting at least 60 minutes. Participants are monitored for infusion-related reactions during infusion and for at least 60 minutes afterward (at least 120 minutes after the first dose).
: Lenvatinib — Continued orally once daily in both cohorts at the tolerated dose used at progression before MRG003 addition. This is continuation of the prior treatment backbone, with protocol-defined dose interruptions, reductions, or discontinuation for safety. MRG003 is added after documented prior clinical benefit and subsequent RECIST 1.1 progression.
: Anti-PD-1 Inhibitor (Continuation of Prior Treatment) — Cohort A only: continue the same anti-PD-1 inhibitor used with lenvatinib when prior clinical benefit and subsequent RECIST 1.1 progression occurred. The agent, dose, route, and dosing schedule follow the participant's prior regimen, with protocol-defined safety adjustments. A different anti-PD-1 inhibitor is not introduced as part of the add-on intervention. MRG003 is the only newly added anticancer agent.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07874321

SUMMARY:

## NCT07875036
Comparative Effects of Coherent Breathing and Regular Paced Breathing on Dyspnea and Pulmonary Function in Patients With Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease

Intervention: : Coherent Breathing Group A — Participants in this group will perform coherent breathing exercises in addition to standard pharmacological management. The intervention will consist of two supervised sessions per week, conducted by a physiotherapist, for a total duration of 4 weeks. Exercises will be performed according to the participant's tolerance and clinical condition.
: Regular Paced Breathing Group B — Regular paced breathing along with pharmacological management. Description: Patients in this group will perform regular paced breathing along with pharmacological management. A total 4 weeks protocol will be followed in which 2 sessions per week will be supervised by physiotherapist and 3 days at home.

Regular Paced Breathing includes:

The patient will be instructed to sit comfortably in an upright position with back supported and feet flat on floor.

Patient will be guided to breathe at a normal, comfortable pace of 12-15 breaths/min. Inhale gently through nose for about 2 seconds. Exhale gently through mouth for about 2-3 seconds. Patients will be instructed not to hold their breath and do not take deep breaths. Participants will be instructed to maintain a record of their daily breathing practices.

It will be performed for 8-10 minutes per session with rest breaks in between.

Pharmacological management: Patient will receive medications as per prescribed by pulmonologist.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07875036

SUMMARY:

## NCT07875049
Additional Effects of Flutter With Conventional Chest Physiotherapy on Peak Expiratory Flow Rate and Chest Expansion in Post CABG Patients With COPD

Intervention: : Conventional Chest Physiotherapy — 1. Diaphragmatic Breathing Description: Slow deep breathing emphasizing diaphragmatic movement performed for10 repetitions × 3 sets per session.
2. Incentive Spirometry Description: Sustained maximal inspiration using an incentive spirometer 10 repetitions × 3 sets per session.
3. Chest Percussion Description: Rhythmic clapping of the chest wall with a cupped, 02-03 minutes on Day 1, 03-05 minutes on Days 2-5.
4. Splinted Coughing Description: Controlled coughing with support to the surgical incision using a pillow or hands to facilitate secretion clearance while minimizing discomfort as needed/occasional during the session.
5. Early Mobilization Description: Gradual progression of physical activity during the postoperative period, including sitting, standing and ambulation, as tolerated.

A 05-day protocol will be followed.
: Flutter device with conventional chest physiotherapy — Flutter therapy will be administered as an adjunct to conventional chest physiotherapy using a portable, oscillating positive expiratory pressure (OPEP) device, for 3 sessions per day for 5 consecutive postoperative days.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07875049

## NCT07875127
Kinematic Analysis of Manual Dexterity and Physiological and Pathological Functional Activity of the Upper Limb in Developmental Age: Pilot Study

Intervention: : Inertial Measurement Unit and Box and Block test — Subjects will be assessed during Standard Box and Block Test, Targeted and Dual Task Box and Block Test with Inertial Measurement Units.

The subject is seated on a stable stool adjustable in height so that the hip and knee angles are formed at 90°. A height-adjustable table is placed in front of the subject: the heights of the table and the seat are adjusted so that the subject forms 90-degree elbow angles by resting the forearms on the table. Once the system has been calibrated and the inertial sensors applied, the patient will be asked to perform the task. Dual Task BBT: the dual task exercise will involve the execution of the BBT, together with the selective naming of the colors. During the transfer of the cubes onto the numbered surface corresponding to their initial position, the participant will be required to focus attention exclusively on the verbalization of the colors of the red and green cubes, without attributing names to the cubes of other colors.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07875127

## NCT07875166
Effectiveness of Enhanced Dialectical Behavior Therapy Skills Training Group for Borderline Personality Disorder: A Pragmatic, Multicenter, Randomized Non-inferiority Trial

Intervention: : Enhanced Dialectical Behavior Therapy Skills Training — The intervention lasts 13 weeks and includes the following components: 1) Introductory Group for Orientation and Distress Tolerance DBT skills (2 hours per week during the first 4 weeks), Since week 2, weekly DBT skills-training group (2.5 hours; 12 weeks), 3) Weekly "check-in" phone calls throughout the 13 weeks, 4) Phone coaching for emergencies during business hours, 5) Weekly consultation team meeting (60.90 minutes)
: Dialectical Behavior Therapy — The intervention begins with a "pretreatment" phase consisting of three to four preparatory individual psychotherapy sessions in which the therapeutic goals are defined, commitment is established, and the patient is oriented to the rules and procedures of therapy. Pretreatment is followed by standard Dialectical Behavior Therapy over 24 weeks, comprising: 1) a weekly DBT skills-training group (2.5 hrs.); 2) weekly individual DBT psychotherapy (45 min.); 3) Access to phone coaching during business hours (although standard DBT considers 24/7 access, this study arm involves only office-hour access plus orientation to using National Hotline); 4) weekly consultation team meeting (60 min.).
: Treatment as Usual (TAU) — It corresponds to the standard treatment currently provided at any community mental health center in Chile, which consists of monthly or fortnightly individual standard psychotherapy sessions.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07875166

Evaluation of Outcomes of Incomplete Repair of Large-to-Massive Rotator Cuff Tears Using Long Head of the Biceps Tendon Transposition and Acellular Dermal Matrix Augmentation

Intervention: : Rotator Cuff Repair — Arthroscopic repair of the posterosuperior rotator cuff to the maximum achievable footprint coverage after release of adhesions, using double-loaded medial-row suture anchors, with margin convergence and footprint medialization (up to 10 mm) when needed.
: Long Head of the Biceps Tendon Transposition — The long head of the biceps tendon is released from the bicipital groove, transposed to the superior greater tuberosity and fixed with a double-loaded suture anchor placed 5 mm lateral to the articular margin, using two lasso-loop stitches.
: Acellular Dermal Matrix (Allograft) Augmentation — A 2-mm-thick acellular dermal matrix graft, sized intraoperatively, is passed over the repair on the medial-row sutures and fixed laterally in a suture-bridge configuration with knotless lateral-row anchors. The device is registered in Vietnam as a Class D medical device (No. 2501939ĐKLH/BYT-HTTB).

## NCT07876245
Efficacy of Interactive Massage Intervention in Toddlers With Autism Spectrum Disorder: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Maternal Interactive Massage — Mothers of participants assigned to the intervention group will receive standardized training in interactive massage, including massage techniques, body areas, intensity, duration, frequency, and safety procedures. Training will include instructional videos and hands-on practice to ensure that mothers can independently and correctly perform the intervention.

The interactive massage will focus on the child's head and face, trunk, limbs, and palms and soles, primarily using gentle stroking, kneading, and pressing techniques. Mothers will perform the massage for 15 minutes per session, three sessions per day, six days per week, for 3 consecutive months.

Intervention adherence will be monitored. Weekly online follow-up and monthly in-person follow-up will be conducted to assess adherence and provide retraining or individualized guidance when needed. An intervention adherence rate of at least
: Behavioral — Participants will receive routine developmental and behavioral pediatric follow-up and standardized ASD family parenting guidance for 3 months. Parenting guidance will include strategies related to social interaction skills and behavior management. No interactive massage or massage-related training will be provided to participants in the usual care group during the study period.

Participants will be randomized in a 1:1 ratio to two ablation-duration strategies. In Group 1, each ablation site in the main renal arteries and their branches will be treated for 1 minute. ## NCT07876648
Prospective Trial of [18F]AlF-FAPI-74 for PET Imaging of Fibroblast Activation Protein in Patients With Endometriosis

Intervention: : [18F]AlF-FAPI-74 PET/CT — A \[18F\]AlF-FAPI-74 PET/CT will be performed at UZ Leuven before the participant's planned laparoscopy. Participants are required to fast for 4 hours before tracer administration but water is allowed. An intravenous line will be placed and \[18F\]AlF-FAPI-74 will be administered in one bolus (3.5 MBq/kg). PET/CT acquisition will start after 60 minutes. The CT will be a low dose CT with oral contrast. If there are no contraindications, 20 mg Buscopan may be administered shortly before imaging to reduce bowel movement.

Participants may optionally undergo an additional NeuroEXPLORER PET/CT scan on the same day after the same \[18F\]AlF-FAPI-74 administration, so no second tracer injection is required. Participants who start treatment with a GnRH (ant)agonist after their first scan may undergo a second \[18F\]AlF-FAPI-74 PET/CT to investigate changes in tracer uptake during hormonal treatment. Participants will be monitored for adverse events related to study procedures.
: [18F]AlF-FAPI-74 PET/CT — A \[18F\]AlF-FAPI-74 PET/CT will be performed at UZ Leuven before the participant's planned laparoscopy. Participants are required to fast for 4 hours before tracer administration but water is allowed. An intravenous line will be placed and \[18F\]AlF-FAPI-74 will be administered in one bolus (3.5 MBq/kg). PET/CT acquisition will start after 60 minutes. The CT will be a low dose CT with oral contrast. If there are no contraindications, 20 mg Buscopan may be administered shortly before imaging to reduce bowel movement.

Participants may optionally undergo an additional NeuroEXPLORER PET/CT scan on the same day after the same \[18F\]AlF-FAPI-74 administration, so no second tracer injection is required. Participants will be monitored for adverse events related to study procedures.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07876648

SUMMARY:

## NCT07876908
An Open-Label Feasibility Trial to Assess the Efficacy of the Magnus Neuromodulation System With Stanford Accelerated Intelligent Neuromodulation Therapy (SAINT)® Technology to Treat Memory Deficits in Older Patients With Treatment-Resistant Depression

Intervention: : Stanford Accelerated Intelligent Neuromodulation Therapy (SAINT®) with the Magnus Neuromodulation System — MRI-guided, individualized accelerated intermittent theta-burst stimulation delivered with the Magnus Neuromodulation System to a personalized left dorsolateral prefrontal cortex target. Participants receive 10 stimulation sessions per day for 5 consecutive days (50 total sessions). Each 10-minute session delivers 1,800 magnetic pulses at 90% of motor threshold with depth adjustment, using 50-Hz triplet bursts at 5 Hz (2-second trains, 8-second inter-train intervals). Sessions are separated by approximately 50 minutes. Total planned exposure is 90,000 pulses. Participants wear hearing protection during stimulation.

Intervention: : Injectable-PRF (I-PRF) — I-PRF will be prepared as follows: (Choukran et al., 2018) i-PRF was prepared by Choukroun's method. A total of 10 mL of venous blood was withdrawn from the patient and was immediately centrifuged for 3 min at 700 rpm in the centrifuge. The liquid in the top of the tube is obtained after centrifugation was collected using a blunt ended syringe. A limited amount of i-PRF was obtained from 10 mL of blood sample (1mL approximately).

The injectable platelet rich fibrin will be injected into the defect Microsurgical suturing technique with 5or 6-0 monofilament polypropylene suture materials will be performed for optimal wound closure of the surgical area.
: sticky bone (I-PRF+Xenograft) — The xenograft will be obtained and mixed with i-PRF. Mixing of xenograft and injectable platelet rich fibrin to get sticky bone graft that will be easily placed into the defect.

The flap will approximated and Microsurgical suturing technique with 5or 6-0 monofilament polypropylene suture materials will be performed for optimal wound closure of the surgical area.

Recorded EEG data will be analyzed for spectral parameters, including alpha power, delta power, alpha/delta power ratio, spectral edge frequency, and frontal alpha asymmetry.

## NCT07877519
Efficacy and Safety of Local Anesthesia With Conscious Sedation Versus General Anesthesia for Pulsed Field Ablation in Patients With Paroxysmal Atrial Fibrillation: A Prospective, Multicenter, Randomized, Controlled, Noninferiority Trial

Intervention: : Local Anesthesia With Conscious Sedation — Protocol-specified antitussive pretreatment is followed by local anesthesia, analgesia, and conscious sedation. Before femoral venous puncture, intravenous tropisetron hydrochloride 5 mg, dexamethasone 5 mg, lidocaine 100 mg over 10 minutes, fentanyl 50 micrograms, midazolam 2 mg, and atropine 1 mg are given. After transseptal puncture, fentanyl 50-100 micrograms is given intravenously, followed by 0.04 micrograms/kg/min infusion. Additional midazolam or fentanyl may be given according to vital signs and clinical tolerance. Conversion to general anesthesia is permitted for persistent severe coughing, pain, anxiety, or agitation interfering with catheter positioning or energy delivery; persistent respiratory depression or refractory hypoxemia; uncorrectable hemodynamic instability; or inability or unwillingness to tolerate the assigned strategy.
: General Anesthesia — General anesthesia will be induced, maintained, and monitored by an anesthesiologist according to routine clinical practice at each participating center. Airway management, anesthetic medications, ventilation parameters, hemodynamic management, and post-anesthesia recovery will be documented.
: VARIPULSE Pulsed Field Ablation System — All participants will undergo bilateral pulmonary vein isolation using the VARIPULSE pulsed field ablation catheter under CARTO 3 electroanatomic mapping guidance. Additional ablation of the left atrial posterior wall, superior vena cava, or other atrial substrate may be performed when clinically indicated. Bidirectional pulmonary vein isolation will be evaluated at the end of the procedure. Activated clotting time will be maintained at 350 seconds or longer during left atrial ablation.
: Compound methoxyphenamine capsules — Oral antitussive pretreatment with compound methoxyphenamine capsules (Asmeton) during the 24 hours before the procedure, as specified in the protocol.
: Salbutamol — Nebulized pretreatment in combination with budesonide and lidocaine within 90 minutes before the procedure, as specified in the protocol for the local anesthesia with conscious sedation arm.
: Budesonide — Nebulized pretreatment in combination with salbutamol and lidocaine within 90 minutes before the procedure, as specified in the protocol for the local anesthesia with conscious sedation arm.
: Lidocaine — Administered by nebulization with salbutamol and budesonide within 90 minutes before the procedure, and intravenously before femoral venous puncture, as specified in the protocol for the local anesthesia with conscious sedation arm.
: Fentanyl — For the local anesthesia with conscious sedation arm: intravenous fentanyl 50 micrograms before femoral venous puncture, followed by 50 to 100 micrograms after transseptal puncture and a continuous intravenous infusion at 0.04 micrograms/kg/min. Additional doses may be administered according to vital signs, pain, and clinical tolerance, as specified in the protocol.
: Midazolam — For the local anesthesia with conscious sedation arm: intravenous midazolam 2 mg before femoral venous puncture. Additional doses may be given according to vital signs and clinical response, as specified in the protocol.
: Tropisetron hydrochloride — Intravenous tropisetron hydrochloride 5 mg before femoral venous puncture as part of the protocol-specified premedication regimen for the local anesthesia with conscious sedation arm.
: Dexamethasone — Intravenous dexamethasone 5 mg before femoral venous puncture as part of the protocol-specified premedication regimen for the local anesthesia with conscious sedation arm.
: Atropine — Intravenous atropine 1 mg before femoral venous puncture as part of the protocol-specified premedication regimen for the local anesthesia with conscious sedation arm.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07877519

## NCT07877935
Testing Interventions to Mitigate the Health Impact of Heatwaves on Primary School Children in Climate-vulnerable Districts of Bangladesh.

Intervention: : Solar reflective paint — Solar reflective paint is a special type of paint used to lower surface temperature in tin-roofs. It works by generating an obstacle between the sun and the tin-roofs. It has ingredients (colour particles) that are designed to reflect ultraviolet (UV) rays of the sun. When it is used in a tin-roof house, it reduces the impact of direct sunlight on the surface of the tin roof and decrease internal temperature of the house.
: Bamboo ceiling with polyethylene foam — Bamboo ceiling combined with polyethylene foam will be utilized for thermal insulation in tin-roof houses. Bamboo, known for its natural insulation properties, acts as a barrier between the tin roof and living space, providing passive cooling. Polyethylene foam further prevents heat transfer, enhancing the insulation. The combination of these materials creates a two-layer system, which is hypothesized to be more effective at reducing indoor temperatures than using either material alone.
: Heat education toolkit — The heat education toolkit will be co-created by the research team with input from students, government and non-government stakeholders, parents, teachers, and healthcare providers. The toolkit will be used in the classrooms to educate students on the negative impacts of heatwaves and how to cope with it. The lessons will be interactive and cover different topics such as what is a heatwave, why heatwaves occur, the effects of heatwaves on the environment and humans, and what can be done to minimize the impacts of heatwaves.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07877935

SUMMARY:

## NCT07877974
The Efficacy of Gastrocnemius Plane Block for Foot and Ankle Surgery: A Randomised Controlled Trial

Intervention: : GPB Group : This group will receive ultrasound-guided Gastrocnemius plane block with 30 mL bupivacaine (0.25%) — o In lateral position, a high frequency US (Siemens ACUSON X300 Ultrasound System) linear probe (8-12 mhz) will be placed in the popliteal fossa facing the operator. Scanning continue towards the foot, and the gastrocnemius will appear. As the scanning progresses towards the caput fibulae, the soleus gradually becomes visible. Once Gastrosoleus Interfascial Plane is identified, a needle advanced by consultant anesthesiologists who had experience in regional anesthesia in a lateral-to-medial direction into the lower surface of the gastrocnemius (between the gastrocnemius and the soleus). Subsequently, 20 mL bupivacaine (0.25%) will be injected to separate the fascias of the two muscles, allowing the local anaesthetic to diffuse around the tibial nerve through the fascial gap. The needle is then withdrawn to the upper surface of the gastrocnemius, and 10 mL bupivacaine (0.25%) will be injected into the upper surface (between gastrocnemius and deep subcutaneous fascia) to block
: SAB — * Patients will receive SAB using 25g spinal needle in the lateral position with rapid crystalloid co-loading. Fifteen milligrams of hyperbaric bupivacaine will be administered at L3-L4 or at L4-L5 interspace in addition to 25 mcg fentanyl. The dose will be reduced to 10 mg in patients aged above 60 years.
* SAB will be considered successful when a bilateral block is confirmed at T12. The block success will be assessed by loss of cold (cold ice) and pain (a 23-gauge needle) sensations. If spinal anaesthesia fails, the patient will receive general anaesthesia and will be excluded from the study.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07877974

## NCT07878286
Association Between Interoceptive Sensitivity and Central Sensitization Symptom Burden in Women With Fibromyalgia: A Cross-Sectional Study

Intervention: : Central Sensitization Inventory — The Central Sensitization Inventory is used by some researchers to investigate pain sensitivity through symptoms associated with central sensitization and related comorbidities. It can help determine the severity of central sensitization. The scale was developed to detect central sensitization in patients with chronic pain and consists of two sections: Section A and Section B. For scoring purposes, only Section A is used. A score of 40 or higher out of 100 is associated with an increased likelihood of central sensitization.
: The Multidimensional Assessment of Interoceptive Awareness scale-2 — The Multidimensional Assessment of Interoceptive Awareness (MAIA) 2 is a 32-item self-report questionnaire assessing eight dimensions of interoceptive awareness. Items are rated from 0 (never) to 5 (always). Each subscale score is calculated as the mean of its items, with higher scores indicating greater awareness in the corresponding dimension. No total score is calculated.
: Revised Fibromyalgia Impact Questionnaire — The Revised Fibromyalgia Impact Questionnaire is composed of 10 items. The first item contains 11 questions related to physical functioning - each question is rated on a 4 point Likert type scale. Items 2 and 3 ask the patient to mark the number of days they felt well and the number of days they were unable to work (including housework) because of fibromyalgia symptoms. Items 4 through 10 are horizontal linear scales marked in 10 increments on which the patient rates work difficulty, pain, fatigue, morning tiredness, stiffness, anxiety and depression. The maximum scale score is 100, with higher scores associated with increasing disability.
: Numeric rating scale — Numeric Rating Scale will be used to determine pain intensity during assessments. Participants will be asked to rate their pain intensity by selecting a value between 0 and 10. Higher scores are associated with increased pain severity.
: Hospital Anxiety and Depression Scale — This scale is used to assess anxiety and depression symptoms and consists of 14 items (7 items for anxiety, 7 items for depression). For each subscale, scores range from 0 to 21: • 0-7 points: No symptoms or very mild symptoms • 8-10 points: Moderate symptoms • 11 points or higher
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07878286

The patient in this arm will have access to the "ThIA Suivi" remote medical monitoring system starting the days following their medical consultation with the psychiatrist and throughout his/her participation in the study, in addition to his/her usual standard of care. ## NCT07878884
Photobiomodulation Therapy (PBMT) Associated With a Pulmonary Rehabilitation Program on the Functional Capacity of Patients With Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease (COPD): a Randomized, Placebo-controlled, Double-blind Clinical Trial.

Intervention: : Intervention Group — Will be calibrated Multi Radience Medical® MR5 ACTIV PRO LaserShower superpulsed laser will be used. Will be applied twice a week for eight weeks to the lower limbs and respiratory muscles. In the lower limb, the following will be irradiated: the quadriceps muscle (4 points), the hamstring muscles (3 points), and the gastrocnemius (1 point), bilaterally. Among the respiratory muscles, the diaphragm (6 points) and the sternocleidomastoid (1 point - bilaterally) will be irradiated. In total, the application will be done at 24 points. The energy transmitted to the patient (dose) will be 30J per application point, through a cluster with four wavelengths (905, 850, 630 and 470 nm), with a frequency of 250hz and a peak pulse power of 200 mW. Considering that each application point receives 30J, the knee extensors are solicited around 240J, the knee flexors and diaphragm 180J, the ankle flexors 60J and the sternocleidomastoid 60J.
: control group — For the application of placebo PBMT, the patient will be instructed to lie on their back and then on their side, and the properly calibrated Multi Radience Medical® MR5 ACTIV PRO LaserShower superpulsed laser will be used. The treatment will be performed with the beam in contact with the skin at a 90º angle. The therapy will be applied twice a week for eight weeks to the lower limbs and respiratory muscles. In the lower limb, the following will be irradiated: the quadriceps muscle (four points), the hamstring muscles (three points), and the gastrocnemius (one point), bilaterally. Among the respiratory muscles, the diaphragm (six points) and the sternocleidomastoid (one point - bilaterally) will be irradiated. In total, the application will be done at 24 points.

In this group, the dose will not be released, however, patients identified the same audible and visual signals, as well as the duration of the device's contact with the skin, as in the treated group.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07878884

SUMMARY:

## NCT07878897
National Highly Specialized Care (NHV) for Adults With Treatment-Resistant Self-Harm and Eating Disorders: Assessment, Development, and Evaluation - Substudy 1: Investigation of Patients' and Their Significant Others' Experiences of National Highly Specialized Care (NHV), Feasibility and Preliminary Treatment Outcomes

Intervention: : National Highly Specialized Care (NHV) — National Highly Specialized Care (NHV) provides individualized treatment for adults with severe and treatment-resistant self-harm or eating disorders. Treatment typically involves approximately 12 weeks of inpatient care or a combination of inpatient and intensive outpatient treatment. Treatment approaches include dialectical behavior therapy (DBT) for self-harm and cognitive behavioral therapy (CBT) or Specialist Supportive Clinical Management (SSCM) for eating disorders. Additional interventions may be provided to address co-occurring psychiatric conditions. Treatment includes preparation and follow-up phases, and its duration and content are adapted to individual clinical needs. Treatment is determined independently of participation in the observational study.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07878897
